planetary albedo & 0.2947 & 0.293 & $+0.0017$& tropics 30S--30N& $+42.6$ & $+21.30$ \\
SW CRE           & $-44.40$ & $-45.4$ & $+1.00$ & 30N--60N     & $-21.8$ & $-3.98$ \\
LW CRE           & $+24.88$ & $+25.8$ & $-0.92$ & 60N--90N     & $-97.7$ & $-6.55$ \\
\textbf{net CRE} & $\mathbf{-19.52}$ & $\mathbf{-19.6}$ & $\mathbf{+0.08}$ & \textbf{sum} & & $\mathbf{+0.67}$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

The cloud radiative effect matches observations to \textbf{$0.08$\,W/m$^2$} and the planetary albedo to
$0.002$. The AMIP and 09C zonal profiles lie almost on top of one another: the coupled model's radiation
\emph{is} the atmosphere's.

\dead{The band contributions reproduce the textbook meridional structure with no band carrying an anomalous
imbalance, so the Hyder et al.\ atmospheric-SW hypothesis does not appear as a gross cloud error and there is
no region to fix.} \textbf{That conclusion was an artifact of the comparison.} The bands above were compared
to \emph{each other}, and only the \emph{global} CRE to CERES. Differencing each band \emph{against CERES}
(\S\ref{sub:sobands}) shows the global agreement is a \textbf{cancellation of large regional errors}, and that
the Southern Ocean carries most of the imbalance.

\begin{figure}[H]\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.99\textwidth]{plots/amip_toa_decomposition_zonal.png}
\caption{Zonal-mean net TOA and cloud radiative effect, AMIP (atmosphere only) vs coupled 09C. The curves are
nearly indistinguishable, and the imbalance is the small residual of a large tropical surplus against
extratropical deficits --- not a localised error.}
\end{figure}

\subsubsection{Differenced against CERES per band: the Southern Ocean carries it}\label{sub:sobands}
\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{l r r r r r}
\toprule
band & model & CERES & diff & area & \textbf{contribution} \\
\midrule
90S--65S              & $-97.0$ & $-104.0$ & $+7.03$ & $4.8\,\%$ & $\mathbf{+0.34}$ \\
\textbf{SO 65S--45S}  & $-49.5$ & $-55.3$  & $\mathbf{+5.81}$ & $9.8\,\%$ & $\mathbf{+0.57}$ \\
45S--30S              & $-5.6$  & $-1.4$   & $-4.20$ & $10.4\,\%$ & $-0.44$ \\
tropics 30S--30N      & $+42.6$ & $+45.1$  & $-2.51$ & $50.0\,\%$ & $-1.26$ \\
30N--45N              & $-4.8$  & $-5.7$   & $+0.89$ & $10.4\,\%$ & $+0.09$ \\
45N--65N              & $-50.1$ & $-54.1$  & $+4.02$ & $9.8\,\%$  & $+0.40$ \\
65N--90N              & $-106.3$& $-106.1$ & $-0.15$ & $4.8\,\%$  & $-0.01$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\textbf{The global budget is right by cancellation, not by being right.} The Southern Ocean
(65--45\textdegree S) runs $+5.8$\,W/m$^2$ too much absorbed energy and contributes
$\mathbf{+0.57}$\,W/m$^2$ to the global mean --- on its own, comparable to the entire $+0.67$ imbalance. The
cause is a cloud \emph{deficit}: cloud area $83.1\,\%$ against CERES's $89.7\,\%$ ($-6.6$\,pp) with SW CRE
$-60.3$ against $-68.1$ ($+7.8$\,W/m$^2$ too little reflection). \textbf{This is precisely the Hyder et al.\
(2018) signature}, and it vindicates the compensating-error worry of \S\ref{sec:so}: the ocean-mixing levers
of Part~I have been removing a Southern-Ocean warm bias that is substantially \emph{atmospheric} in origin.

\textbf{Consequence for the plan.} The $+0.67$ is not irreducible after all --- but neither is it fixable by a
global knob, since the global mean is already correct. It is fixable \emph{regionally}, and the two largest
regional errors have \emph{opposite} sign at TOA, which is what makes the round-10 design work
(\S\ref{sec:round10}). Re-framing the campaign arithmetic:
\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{l r c r}
\toprule
 & net TOA & $=$ atmosphere $+$ & coupled-state \\
\midrule
080a (untuned coupled) & 1.44 & $0.67\ +$ & $\mathbf{0.77}$ \\
09C (06V $+$ newSeaIce) & 0.92 & $0.67\ +$ & $\mathbf{0.25}$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The ocean-side tuning has already delivered about two-thirds of what was available to it. Whether the HR
piControl goal of $-0.16$ is even the right target for this configuration should be revisited, given that
$\approx0.67$ of any coupled value is an atmosphere matching observations.
\emph{Revisited and answered on 2026--08--07: it is not the right target, and the goal is now $0$
(\S\ref{sec:toagoal}).}

\subsection{Result II --- the boreal \emph{summer} cold bias is atmospheric}\label{sub:amipsummer}
Monthly 2\,m-T bias against CRUNCEP3 (1901--10), land only, cos-lat weighted:

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{l r r r}
\toprule
series & Boreal JJA/ann & Siberia JJA/ann & E.\ Siberia JJA/ann \\
\midrule
\textbf{AMIP (atmosphere only)} & $-1.64$ / $-1.05$ & $\mathbf{-2.16}$ / $-0.89$ & $\mathbf{-2.24}$ / $-0.49$ \\
080a (baseline, old sea ice)    & $-0.36$ / $-0.90$ & $-1.41$ / $-1.36$ & $-1.80$ / $-1.48$ \\
09A (baseline + newSeaIce)      & $-1.24$ / $-2.06$ & $-2.19$ / $-3.00$ & $-2.47$ / $-2.89$ \\
09B (06T + newSeaIce)           & $-1.56$ / $-2.33$ & $-2.50$ / $-3.11$ & $-2.72$ / $-3.04$ \\
09C (06V + newSeaIce)           & $-1.93$ / $-2.64$ & $-2.54$ / $-3.08$ & $-2.69$ / $-3.05$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

The atmosphere alone reproduces essentially all of 09A's Siberian JJA bias ($-2.16$ vs $-2.19$) and most of
09B/09C's. \textbf{Removing the ocean and LPJ-GUESS does not remove the summer cold bias.} The cold-season
half behaves oppositely --- AMIP's Siberian \emph{annual} bias is only $-0.89$ against $-3.0$ coupled --- so
September--April remains the coupled/vegetation problem of Part~II, and JJA is now a separate, atmospheric one.

\subsubsection{Correction (2026--07--29), \textcolor{red}{itself superseded 2026--07--30}: the reference period matters, but far less than estimated}\label{sub:perioffset}
\textbf{Read \S\ref{sub:perdirect} first.} The indirect estimate in this subsection put the Siberian JJA period offset at $\approx1.12$\,K and concluded the genuine boreal bias was $\approx-1.0$\,K. The \texttt{amip\_presentday} run has since \emph{measured} that offset: $\mathbf{+0.42}$\,\textbf{K}. The conclusions below are therefore \textbf{withdrawn} --- the boreal bias is $\approx-2.0$\,K, close to the original figure. The reasoning is retained because the method error is instructive: it applied an \emph{observed} epoch change to a model that does not reproduce that change.
The number above is scored against \textbf{ERA5 1990--2014}, while these runs use \textbf{1870s observed SST
with 1850 GHG}. Roughly $130$ years of greenhouse warming therefore sits inside a quantity the campaign has
been treating as model error --- and tuning against. Measured from the DKRZ ERA5 pool
(\texttt{scripts/analysis/era5\_period\_offset.sh}), Siberian JJA over the same box used for evaluation:

\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{lr}
\toprule
ERA5 period & offset vs 1990--2014 \\
\midrule
1979--1989 & $\mathbf{-0.670}$\,K \\
1940--1969 & $\mathbf{-0.790}$\,K \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent The interannual sd is $0.578$\,K, giving SE $=0.209$\,K on the 1979--89 vs 1990--2014 difference
($t\approx3.2$): the offset is real. ERA5 stops at 1940, so chaining through HadCRUT5's global series
(1870s $\to$ 1990--2014 $= +0.756$\,K) scaled by ERA5's own Siberian-JJA-to-global amplification over the
overlapping window ($\times1.48$) implies a Siberian JJA period offset of $\approx1.12$\,K, leaving a
\textbf{genuine model cold bias of $\approx-1.0$\,K rather than $-2.2$\,K}.

\textbf{This changes the target, not the diagnosis.} The attribution above is unaffected --- it is a
model-versus-model comparison (AMIP against coupled 09A) in which any reference offset cancels, so ``the JJA
bias is atmospheric'' stands. What changes is \emph{how much} of it we should try to remove: closing the full
$2.2$\,K would manufacture a $\approx1$\,K \emph{warm} bias in the coupled pre-industrial state.

\emph{Caveats.} The ERA5 box is unmasked whereas the model figures are land-masked; land warms faster than
ocean, so the true land-only offset is if anything larger and this correction is conservative. The $\times1.48$
amplification is extrapolated back from a single window, and HadCRUT5's 1870s global mean rests on sparse
coverage. \textbf{The same flaw applies to the CERES-based energy targets} (\S\ref{sub:ceres},
\S\ref{sub:sobands}): CERES EBAF here is the 07/2005--06/2015 climatology scored against an 1870s model, so
the $+8.08$\,W/m$^2$ SO SW CRE gap and the $+0.53$\,W/m$^2$ imbalance carry an analogous, \emph{as yet
unquantified}, period offset. The decisive fix for both is a single present-day AMIP leg (1989--2015,
transient GHG) scored directly against ERA5 1990--2014 and CERES 2005--2015; the AMIP SST forcing already
covers 187001--201512.

\subsubsection{Attribution (2026--07--29): the boreal forest is a \emph{temperature} problem}\label{sub:radvstemp}
The forcing-transfer test (\S\ref{sub:xfer}) showed that swapping CRUNCEP\,$\to$\,AMIP forcing alone costs
$-54$\,\% of Siberian \texttt{TREEFPC}, but could not say \emph{which} variable carried it: radiation and
temperature both feed NPP, and therefore both feed the \texttt{greff\_min} growth-efficiency mortality that
\S\ref{sub:decisive} identifies as the actual killer (\S\ref{sub:decisive}). A colleague's spin-ups separate them by holding CRUNCEP
temperature fixed and swapping \emph{only} the radiation to CERES
(\texttt{LPJG-SPINUP\_2000Y\_TCO95\_CORE3\_CRUNCEPandCERES}). \textbf{\texttt{AGDD5} is identical between
those two arms} ($775.8$ Siberia, $748.0$ E.\ Siberia, $1157.5$ NH) --- the control confirming that only
radiation moved.

\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{lrrr}
\toprule
region & radiation only & full swap & radiation share \\
 & (CRUNCEP$\to$CERES) & (CRUNCEP$\to$AMIP) & \\
\midrule
NH 45N+     & $-1.7$\,\%  & $-25.1$\,\% & $\mathbf{7}$\,\% \\
Siberia     & $-8.9$\,\%  & $-54.3$\,\% & $\mathbf{16}$\,\% \\
E.\ Siberia & $-10.5$\,\% & $-66.0$\,\% & $\mathbf{16}$\,\% \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent The radiation arm moves $-21$\,W/m$^2$ while the full swap moves $\approx-32$, so scaling linearly
puts radiation at $\le25$\,\%. The daily-variability variant is indistinguishable ($-7.8$ vs $-8.9$\,\%), so
it is not a sub-daily distribution effect either. \textbf{Temperature carries $\approx75$--$85$\,\% of the
boreal tree collapse.}

\textbf{Consequence: prong~A's radiation work will not restore the forest.} Closing the \emph{entire} Siberian
SW deficit recovers at most a quarter of the tree loss. The forest depends on the $\approx1.0$\,K temperature
bias established in \S\ref{sub:perioffset}, not on the Southern-Ocean/energy target. These are two separate
jobs and should stop being described as one.

\textbf{The period mismatch also sits on the LPJG side.} CRUNCEP3 spans \textbf{1901--2015} --- twentieth-century
climate --- yet was used to spin up the \emph{pre-industrial} vegetation state that the coupled 1850 model
restarts from. Part of the $131$-\texttt{GDD5} Siberian gap is therefore a real PI-versus-20thC climate
difference rather than model error: $131$\,\texttt{GDD5} over a $\approx120$-day season is $\approx1.1$\,K, of
which the 1870s\,$\to$\,1901--2015 offset plausibly accounts for $0.3$--$0.45$\,K, leaving $0.6$--$0.8$\,K
genuine --- consistent with the $\approx1.0$\,K derived against ERA5 1990--2014, since CRUNCEP's period is
cooler than ERA5's. \emph{Caveat:} the AMIP arm used the superseded forcing build, $\approx0.9$\,K warmer over
Siberian JJA, so the temperature share is if anything understated.

\textbf{Does this shift the residual onto LPJ-GUESS's CRUNCEP calibration?} Partly, but the corroboration
attempted on the \emph{temperature} axis fails: NCEP2 Siberian JJA is $-0.118$\,K \emph{colder} than ERA5 over
1990--2014 (paired by year, $t=-2.6$) --- real but negligible, and the wrong sign for a story in which
LPJ-GUESS grew its trees under an over-warm forcing. That is consistent with CRUNCEP3's construction:
temperature derives from CRU, bias-corrected against stations, while \emph{radiation} derives from NCEP, which
is precisely where the $+21$\,W/m$^2$ excess against CERES was measured (\S\ref{sub:ceres}). The calibration
mismatch sits on the radiation axis. The direct evidence remains the forcing-transfer test
(\S\ref{sub:xfer}), which does not depend on which variable carries it.

\begin{figure}[H]\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.99\textwidth]{plots/campaign_boreal_seasonal_bias.png}
\caption{Boreal 2\,m-T bias by month against CRUNCEP3, land-masked. The atmosphere-only AMIP curve (blue)
sits at or below the coupled runs through JJA (shaded) but far above them in winter: \textbf{the summer
bias is atmospheric, the cold-season bias is not}. \emph{Caveat:} the ``Boreal 55--70\textdegree N'' panel is
contaminated by the Greenland ice sheet (JJA snow depth $320$\,mm w.e.\ in that box vs $5.5$\,mm for
Siberia); the Siberia and E.\ Siberia panels are clean and carry the argument.}
\end{figure}

\textbf{Superseded numbers.} \dead{AMIP Siberia JJA $-1.23$, E.\ Siberia $-1.59$, i.e.\ indistinguishable from
the coupled baseline, so coupling adds nothing in summer.} Those came from the earlier
\texttt{amip\_pi\_forcing} run, built on the wrong OIFS branch and with \texttt{TMPRALBSEAD}$=0.075$. The
corrected build is $\approx0.9$\,K colder in Siberian JJA; the conclusion strengthens (the atmosphere is now
\emph{colder} in summer than the coupled baseline) but the numbers changed.

\section{Round 09 --- new sea-ice thermodynamics (2026--07--27/28)}\label{sec:round09}
FESOM 2.7 \texttt{awiesm3-implicit-ice-surftemp}\,@\,\texttt{db7dd9af} makes the ice
surface-temperature solve implicit (d$Q$/d$T$-linearised about the last transmitted \texttt{ist}) and hands
ownership of the ice surface energy balance to OpenIFS, which now runs a slab-conduction ice tile using
FESOM-supplied thickness; FESOM ice growth becomes $Q_\mathrm{atmice}=-a2ihf$. The pairing is verifiable at
runtime in the OASIS \texttt{namcouple}: the 09 runs carry a sixth o2a field,
\texttt{sit\_feom}$\to$\texttt{A\_Ice\_thickness}, absent from every 08 run.
Run identities and the 09B parameter block are catalogued in the 2026--07--28 update at the head of this
report; the quantitative comparison is below. \texttt{09C} was built and run on our own tree
(\texttt{Tuning\_test\_09C\_06V\_CRUNCEPinit\_newSeaIce}) to close the missing 06V corner.

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{l r r r r}
\toprule
run & net TOA [W/m$^2$] & global T2m [\textdegree C] & Siberia JJA [K] & Siberia ann [K] \\
\midrule
080a baseline, old sea ice & 1.442 & 13.606 & $-1.41$ & $-1.36$ \\
09A baseline + newSeaIce   & 1.286 & 13.148 & $-2.19$ & $-3.00$ \\
09B 06T + newSeaIce        & 1.171 & 12.794 & $-2.50$ & $-3.11$ \\
08B 06V, old sea ice       & 0.935 & 12.347 & $-3.11$ & $-4.09$ \\
\textbf{09C 06V + newSeaIce} & \textbf{0.919} & 12.492 & $\mathbf{-2.54}$ & $\mathbf{-3.08}$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

On the clean old-vs-new 06V pair (08B\,$\to$\,09C) the radiation barely moves ($0.935\to0.919$) but the
boreal \textbf{warms by $+1.0$\,K in the Siberian annual mean}, $+0.57$\,K in JJA, $+0.15$\,K globally.
\textbf{09C dominates 08B on both objectives simultaneously} --- the first time in the campaign that has
happened, and the ``free warming'' sought in \S\ref{sec:rec}. But the effect is \emph{not} an offset: on the
baseline (080a\,$\to$\,09A) the same code change goes the other way, cooling $0.46$\,K globally and $1.6$\,K
in the Siberian annual mean. The sign depends on the ocean-mixing branch; no mechanism is established here,
and pinning it down needs the surface energy budget over ice.

\begin{figure}[H]\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.92\textwidth]{plots/campaign_net_toa_by_year.png}
\caption{Annual global-mean net TOA (thin) with 5-yr running mean (bold), common 1350--1379 window. The
dotted line is the \textbf{atmosphere-only floor} measured by AMIP ($+0.67$\,W/m$^2$); the shaded band is the
only part of the remaining gap that ocean-side tuning can still close. Everything above the dotted line is
atmospheric.}
\end{figure}

\emph{Bookkeeping caveat.} \texttt{sacct} shows a \texttt{Tuning\_test\_08A\_06T\_1hCPL\_MOSPP\_KPPLOW\_CRUNCEPinit}
completing all three chunks on 24--25 July, but no 08A directory exists on disk. 08A was the 06T-only
physical control --- Run~1 of the 2026-07-24 design note's own sequence --- so within round 08 every
surviving 06T-family entry (08C, 08E, 08G) carries an LPJ lever and the intended 06T-vs-06V comparison
cannot be made cleanly. Round 09 supersedes this, but conclusions drawn from round 08 alone inherit the hole.

\section{Why the summer bias exists --- a shortwave deficit (2026--07--28)}\label{sec:jja}
Siberia JJA surface energy budget, land-masked, cos-lat weighted (AMIP 1872--79 vs CRUNCEP3 1901--10, which
carries \texttt{rsns}/\texttt{rlns} and so permits a direct radiative comparison):

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{l r r r}
\toprule
 & AMIP & CRUNCEP3 & $\Delta$ \\
\midrule
2\,m temperature      & $9.74$\,\textdegree C & $11.92$\,\textdegree C & $\mathbf{-2.18}$\,K \\
SW net at surface     & $155.1$ & $188.3$ & $\mathbf{-33.2}$\,W/m$^2$ \\
LW net at surface     & $-49.2$ & $-64.5$ & $+15.2$\,W/m$^2$ \\
\midrule
net radiation         &         &         & $\mathbf{-18}$\,W/m$^2$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

Supporting AMIP diagnostics: SW$\downarrow$ $188.6$, surface albedo $0.178$ (\texttt{fal} $0.172$),
LW$\downarrow$ $315.6$, SH/LH $-24.6/-56.8$ (\textbf{Bowen $0.43$}), cloud \texttt{tcc} $0.778$ /
\texttt{lcc} $0.505$ / \texttt{mcc} $0.386$, \texttt{skt}$-$\texttt{2t} $+0.43$\,K, \texttt{swvl1} $0.343$,
snow depth $5.5$\,mm w.e.

The LW term is \emph{compensation} (a colder, cloudier surface loses less longwave), not cause. It is
\emph{not} albedo: a realistic boreal-summer $0.13$--$0.15$ instead of the model's $0.178$ buys only
$5$--$9$\,W/m$^2$. So SW$\downarrow$ itself is too low. This is a different mechanism from the winter
stable-BL inversion of \S\ref{sub:residual}, and it is not addressed by any lever proposed so far.

\subsection{Re-measured against CERES: cloud, not clear-sky --- and three times smaller}\label{sub:ceres}
CRUNCEP3 is a reanalysis-derived forcing product, so the deficit was re-measured against
\textbf{CERES EBAF Ed4.1} (satellite), which supplies all-sky \emph{and} clear-sky net SW at the surface and
so permits the decomposition
$\Delta(\mathrm{SW}_\mathrm{net})=\Delta(\mathrm{SW}_\mathrm{net,clear})+\Delta(\mathrm{SW\ CRE}_\mathrm{sfc})$
with no assumption about surface albedo or the direct/diffuse split.

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{l r r r r r r}
\toprule
JJA & \multicolumn{3}{c}{Siberia land} & \multicolumn{3}{c}{NH 55--75\textdegree N ocean} \\
\cmidrule(lr){2-4}\cmidrule(lr){5-7}
 & model & CERES & diff & model & CERES & diff \\
\midrule
SW net at surface, all-sky   & 155.1 & 166.3 & $\mathbf{-11.2}$ & 142.4 & 155.4 & $-13.0$ \\
SW net at surface, clear-sky & 246.6 & 241.4 & $+5.3$           & 241.9 & 252.6 & $-10.7$ \\
SW CRE at surface            & $-91.6$ & $-75.1$ & $\mathbf{-16.5}$ & $-99.5$ & $-97.3$ & $\mathbf{-2.2}$ \\
cloud area [\%]              & 77.8 & 69.6 & $\mathbf{+8.2}$   & 84.6 & 83.0 & $\mathbf{+1.6}$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

Three results, all of which revise \S\ref{sec:jja}:

\textbf{(i) It is unambiguously a cloud error.} The decomposition closes exactly
($-11.2=+5.3-16.5$), and clear-sky surface SW is if anything slightly \emph{too bright}. Aerosol, water
vapour and surface albedo are therefore all exonerated --- and since PI aerosol is lower than present-day, the
PI-vs-CERES period mismatch works \emph{against} this finding, making it conservative.

\textbf{(ii) The deficit is $11$\,W/m$^2$, not $33$.}
\dead{Surface net SW over Siberian land in JJA is 33\,W/m$^2$ too low.} That figure was measured against
CRUNCEP3, which is itself $\approx21$\,W/m$^2$ brighter than satellite --- \textbf{CRUNCEP3 was the outlier,
not the model}. An $11$\,W/m$^2$ summer deficit remains the right order to explain a $\approx2$\,K land cold
bias, so the mechanism survives; only its magnitude changes.

\emph{Verified independently}, because the original comparison had assembled the two sides on different grids
with different land masks --- a way to manufacture exactly this kind of difference. Redone with all three
sources on the CERES $1^\circ$ grid under a single land mask (\texttt{verify\_ceres\_cruncep.py}): CERES
$166.7$, AMIP $155.8$ ($-10.8$), CRUNCEP3 $187.6$ ($+21.0$). Raising the land-mask threshold $0.5\to0.8$
changes these by $<1$\,W/m$^2$, and CERES's own JJA-mean file gives $168.4$ against $166.7$ from the monthly
climatology. The grid inconsistency was therefore not the cause.

\emph{Why CRUNCEP3 is bright} splits cleanly by season: in JJA it is the downward flux
($234$\,W/m$^2$ in July against CERES's $209$, the NCEP high-latitude brightness bias), whereas in spring it
is the forcing file's fixed albedo (\S\ref{sub:ruledout}). CRUNCEP3 blends CRU \emph{station} temperature with
NCEP \emph{reanalysis} radiation, which is the natural reading of why its T2m is usable while its SW is not.

\begin{figure}[H]\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.99\textwidth]{plots/verify_ceres_amip_cruncep.png}
\caption{Surface net shortwave against CERES EBAF, all sources on a common $1^\circ$ grid with one land mask.
AMIP (orange) is $\approx11$\,W/m$^2$ \emph{dark} in JJA; the CRUNCEP forcing (green) is $\approx21$\,W/m$^2$
\emph{bright} in JJA and far more so in spring, where its fixed albedo does not mask snow. The two forcings
differ from each other by $\approx32$\,W/m$^2$ in the growing season --- the transfer step of
\S\ref{sec:calibmismatch}.}
\end{figure}

\textbf{(iii) It is specific to boreal land.} Over ocean in the same latitude band the cloud error nearly
vanishes ($-2.2$\,W/m$^2$ CRE, $+1.6$\,pp area) while over Siberian land it is $-16.5$ and $+8.2$. A
\emph{global} cloud knob would therefore move the ocean, where nothing is wrong, and spend TOA budget for no
benefit. The error is over land, in summer --- pointing at surface-driven convection and boundary-layer cloud
rather than the large-scale cloud scheme.

\textbf{Amount or opacity?} Both, roughly half each. At the observed CRE per unit cloud area
($-75.1/0.696\approx-108$\,W/m$^2$), the $+8.2$\,pp excess accounts for $\approx-8.8$ of the $-16.5$\,W/m$^2$
CRE error; the residual $\approx-7.7$ is opacity or vertical placement. A cloud-\emph{amount} lever alone
therefore reaches at most half of it.

\subsection{Does the cloud fix conflict with the energy target? Less than first thought}\label{sub:jjaknob}
\dead{Burning off that cloud stops reflecting it at TOA, costing $\approx+0.18$\,W/m$^2$ on the annual global
TOA --- a $27\,\%$ worsening --- so a pure cloud fix cannot meet a fixed-TOA constraint, and only a
radiatively neutral surface-flux lever can.} Two things falsify that framing:

\begin{itemize}\itemsep1pt
\item \textbf{The target is the \emph{surface}, not TOA} (\S\ref{sub:snowenth}), and the drift it controls is
driven by the flux into the \emph{ocean}. The cloud error is over \emph{land}
(\S\ref{sub:ceres}), where annual-mean energy storage is $\approx0$ --- land returns what it receives. A
boreal-land cloud correction therefore barely touches the quantity that governs spin-up drift.
\item \textbf{The error is land-specific}, so the correction need not be a global knob at all
(\S\ref{sub:ceres}) --- which was the whole reason a TOA cost was incurred in the first estimate.
\end{itemize}
The two objectives are consequently far more separable than the original framing implied, and the cloud
error can be attacked at its diagnosed cause rather than through a proxy.

\subsection{The stomatal-resistance sensitivity test (experiment A)}\label{sub:expA}
A second, radiatively neutral lever was nonetheless identified and is being tested, because the measured
Bowen ratio over Siberian JJA land is $\mathrm{SH/LH}=-24.6/-56.8=\mathbf{0.43}$, low against the general
observed picture for boreal conifer forest. Minimum canopy resistance for boreal needleleaf
(\texttt{susveg\_mod.F90}:266--267) is $250$\,s/m, with $500$ retained just above as a comment.

\textbf{Provenance --- and a correction.} \dead{$500$ is the IFS default and has been halved in this build.}
Git refutes this: both the active $250$ and the commented $500$ are present in
commit \texttt{cf82033}, ``initial commit of 48r1 released version'', and no AWI commit has ever touched
\texttt{RVRSMIN}. \textbf{$250$ is what ECMWF ships in 48r1}; $500$ is the older HTESSEL-era value they
themselves revised. Setting $500$ moves \emph{away} from the released configuration, so this is a
\textbf{sensitivity test, not a default restoration}.

\emph{Status:} \texttt{amip\_expA\_rvrsmin500}, 6\,yr, paired against \texttt{amip\_pi\_base} on 1872--1875.
Verified compiled in by inspecting the rebuilt object file (the value appears twice for types 3--4; $250$
survives only for Desert and Mixed Forest). Note this tests \emph{partitioning}, not the diagnosed cause: if
the cloud error is real then a resistance change compensates for it, and with $11$\,W/m$^2$ less available
energy there is also less to repartition. \emph{Caveat:} ``Bowen $0.43$ is too low'' is inferred from the
literature's general picture, not measured --- neither CRUNCEP3 nor CERES supplies SH/LH here, and FLUXNET
boreal sites would settle it.

\textbf{A connection worth testing in the same run.} Excess summer evapotranspiration over boreal land would
\emph{both} cool the surface directly \emph{and} moisten the boundary layer, feeding the excess low cloud
(model \texttt{lcc}$=50.5\,\%$ over Siberian land in JJA). If cutting transpiration also reduces low cloud,
the partitioning error and the cloud error are the same problem, and experiment A tests both at once.

\section{Forcing transfer: the spin-up was grown under brighter, warmer conditions than the model delivers}\label{sec:calibmismatch}
The vegetation state every coupled run in rounds 08 and 09 restarts from is the standalone offline spin-up
\texttt{LPJG-SPINUP\_2000Y\_TCO95\_CORE3\_CRUNCEPcalibrated\_v3}
(\texttt{/work/bb1469/a270270/runtime/lpjg-spinup/}), driven by
\texttt{CRUNCEP\_noLPJG\_1d\_1901-1910\_TCO95\_calibrated\_v3.nc} (verified at
\texttt{lpj\_guess\_levante\_spinup\_...\_2000y.yaml}:38). \textbf{That forcing and the atmosphere the coupled
model provides differ substantially, and in the same direction for both light and temperature.}

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{l r r}
\toprule
Siberia land, JJA & surface net SW [W/m$^2$] & vs CERES \\
\midrule
CERES EBAF (observed)                       & 166.7 & --- \\
\textbf{CRUNCEP forcing} (what LPJG grew under) & \textbf{187.6} & $\mathbf{+21}$ ($+13\,\%$) \\
\textbf{AMIP} (what our atmosphere delivers)    & \textbf{155.8} & $\mathbf{-11}$ ($-7\,\%$) \\
\midrule
\multicolumn{2}{l}{\textbf{gap between the two forcings}} & $\mathbf{32}$\,W/m$^2$ ($\approx20\,\%$) \\
\multicolumn{2}{l}{\textbf{plus 2\,m temperature}} & $\mathbf{2.2}$\,K (11.9 vs 9.7\,\textdegree C) \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

The errors point \emph{opposite} ways and therefore compound: CRUNCEP is too bright (the NCEP high-latitude
brightness bias --- its downward SW is $234$\,W/m$^2$ in July against CERES's $209$, $\approx12\,\%$ high),
while our atmosphere is too dark by the cloud error of \S\ref{sub:ceres}. Vegetation equilibrated to the
first and then handed the second experiences a $\approx20\,\%$ drop in growing-season radiation and a
$2.2$\,K cooling --- a step change in exactly the direction that pushes a boreal system from trees toward
grasses. All numbers here are computed on a common CERES $1^\circ$ grid with a single land mask
(\texttt{verify\_ceres\_cruncep.py}); mask threshold $0.5\to0.8$ moves them by $<1$\,W/m$^2$.

\subsection{Ruled out by measurement}\label{sub:ruledout}
Several components were suspected along the way and cleared. Recording them so the same ground is not
re-covered:

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{l l}
\toprule
suspect & the observation that cleared it \\
\midrule
LPJ-GUESS core radiation handling &
\dead{PAR is inflated because no albedo is applied.} Reading \texttt{rsns} as
\texttt{NETSWRAD\_TS} and applying \emph{no} further albedo is \textbf{correct} for a net-flux \\
& input (\texttt{driver.cpp}:1435--1449); a second correction would double-count. Model behaves as designed. \\[2pt]
EC-Earth spin-up interface &
Reads the right variable and sets the right type (\texttt{framework.cpp}:2501--2507). No fault found. \\[2pt]
The forcing script's fixed albedo &
\dead{\texttt{rsns}$=$\texttt{FSDS}$\times0.861$ is a fixed 0.139 albedo, so spring PAR is inflated $\times1.66$
and the spin-up is undermined.} \\
& True that the albedo is fixed (ratio $0.861$ in every cell, April and July alike). But in JJA that gives \\
& $0.139$ against CERES's $\mathbf{0.142}$ --- right to $0.003$. The large spring error
($0.139$ vs $0.535$ in April) \\
& is \textbf{biologically inert}: measured over 687 Siberian cells at equilibrium, April carries
$\mathbf{1.1\,\%}$ of annual \\
& GPP and April LAI is $0.02$ (no canopy), while \textbf{JJA carries $\mathbf{80\,\%}$}. Weighted by when carbon \\
& is actually assimilated, the PAR excess is $\approx13\,\%$, not $66\,\%$. \\[2pt]
\dead{...and it is an unusual, campaign-specific defect} &
Standalone LPJ-GUESS assumes a constant albedo natively: \texttt{BETA}$=0.17$
(\texttt{driver.cpp}:1320), applied \\
& in both the sunshine and \texttt{SWRAD} paths. The script's $0.139$ is a slightly darker version of \\
& standard practice, and switching to its \texttt{bulk\_albedo} option would give $0.195$ in JJA --- \\
& \emph{worse} than the fixed value. \textbf{Do not ``fix'' the script; it is not the problem.} \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\subsection{What survives, and how to settle it}\label{sub:forcingtest}
What remains is not a defect in any component but a \textbf{transfer problem}: a $\approx20\,\%$
growing-season radiation step and a $2.2$\,K temperature step between the forcing the vegetation
equilibrated to and the forcing it is then asked to live in. \textbf{Whether LPJG's \emph{parameters} were
tuned against CRUNCEP --- the ``calibrated'' in the directory name --- is not established}; the 07-series did
change \texttt{greff\_min} and \texttt{pstemp\_low}, but no evidence was found of what was calibrated against
what, and the folder name is not proof.

\textbf{It still weakens the ``competition, not climate'' verdict of \S\ref{sub:decisive}}, though less than
first argued. That test moved a standalone re-spin from the CRUNCEP forcing to the OIFS--AMIP forcing and
read the resulting under-vegetation as an LPJG-internal competition failure. But that move alone removes
$\approx20\,\%$ of the growing-season light and $2.2$\,K, so under-vegetation is partly what the forcing
change predicts on its own. The verdict should be read as provisional. (The forcing used there was also the
\emph{superseded} AMIP build, \S\ref{sub:amipsummer}.)

\subsection{The transfer test, measured: forcing alone halves boreal tree cover}\label{sub:xfer}
The test needs no knowledge of what was tuned --- take the \emph{same} LPJG parameters and spin to
equilibrium under each forcing; the difference \emph{is} the transfer penalty. \textbf{Both arms already
existed} and required no new computation:
\begin{center}\footnotesize
\begin{tabular}{l l}
\toprule
CRUNCEP arm & \texttt{lpjg-spinup/LPJG-SPINUP\_2000Y\_TCO95\_CORE3\_CRUNCEPcalibrated\_v3} \\
AMIP arm    & \texttt{lpjg-spinup-develop/LR\_2000y\_PIforcing\_v3} \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Controlled: \texttt{global.ins} \textbf{byte-identical} between the two trees, \texttt{firemodel "GLOBFIRM"}
in both \texttt{.ins} files, \texttt{nyear\_spinup}$=2000$ / \texttt{freenyears}$=100$, fixed 1850
CO$_2$/Ndep/LU, both reaching year 3900, and \textbf{10\,043 gridcells in common} (99.8\,\% of the AMIP
gridlist). The material difference is the forcing file.

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{l l r r r r}
\toprule
region & & CRUNCEP-forced & AMIP-forced & diff & \% \\
\midrule
\textbf{Siberia} & \texttt{TREEFPC}  & 0.357 & \textbf{0.163} & $-0.194$ & $\mathbf{-54}$ \\
                 & \texttt{GRASSFPC} & 0.288 & 0.330 & $+0.042$ & $+15$ \\
                 & \texttt{AGDD5}    & 776 & 645 & $-131$ & $-17$ \\
\midrule
\textbf{E.\ Siberia} & \texttt{TREEFPC}  & 0.359 & \textbf{0.122} & $-0.236$ & $\mathbf{-66}$ \\
                     & \texttt{GRASSFPC} & 0.316 & 0.398 & $+0.082$ & $+26$ \\
                     & \texttt{AGDD5}    & 748 & 568 & $-180$ & $-24$ \\
\midrule
NH 45\textdegree N$+$ & \texttt{TREEFPC} & 0.383 & 0.287 & $-0.096$ & $-25$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\textbf{Changing nothing but the forcing halves the Siberian tree fraction and cuts East Siberia's by
two-thirds, with grass taking up the space.} For scale, the coupled 080a run lost \texttt{TREEFPC}
$0.310\to0.121$ over Siberia in 50 years (\S\ref{sub:cruncep3_init_followup}); the forcing swap alone
reproduces essentially that entire loss at equilibrium, with no parameter changed. \texttt{AGDD5} falls
$17$--$24\,\%$ in the same direction, so this is not a light effect alone --- the $2.2$\,K also does real work
through the growing-degree-day gates.

\begin{figure}[H]\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.99\textwidth]{plots/forcing_transfer_test.png}
\caption{Zonal means at equilibrium (yr 3900), 10\,043 common gridcells, byte-identical \texttt{global.ins}.
South of $\approx52$\textdegree N the two arms are indistinguishable; northward they diverge sharply and the
AMIP-forced treeline retreats several degrees south. The deficit the campaign has been attributing to
competition is reproduced by the forcing change alone.}
\end{figure}

\textbf{Three caveats, all material.}
\begin{enumerate}\itemsep1pt
\item The arms were built from \emph{different binaries} (a270092 vs a270270) of the same LPJG version with
identical \texttt{global.ins}. A compiled difference cannot be excluded; re-running both arms from one build
($\approx3$\,h) would close it, and should be done before this is published.
\item The AMIP forcing is the \emph{superseded} build (\S\ref{sub:amipsummer}), $\approx0.9$\,K warmer over
Siberian JJA land than the corrected one --- so this is a \textbf{lower bound} on the penalty against today's
atmosphere.
\item \dead{\texttt{FORESTFPC}$=0$ in both arms, so the absence of closed canopy survives as a separate
unexplained problem.} \texttt{FORESTFPC} is a \emph{void diagnostic} in this configuration --- it counts only
the managed-forest land-use class, which is empty here, so it is identically zero regardless of what the
vegetation does. There is no separate closed-canopy failure to explain.
\end{enumerate}

\section{Revised plan: tune the atmosphere on AMIP}\label{sec:amipplan}
Both errors that remain --- $\approx0.83$\,W/m$^2$ of TOA imbalance (\S\ref{sub:amiptoa}) and
$\approx2.2$\,K of Siberian summer cold (\S\ref{sub:amipsummer}) --- are atmospheric, and both are now
\emph{measured} in a configuration where neither the ocean nor LPJ-GUESS can confound the answer. They should
therefore be tuned on AMIP, not in coupled mode. This does not retire Phases~1A/1B of \S\ref{sec:workplan}
--- the LPJG competition fix and the stable-BL mixing fix still target the \emph{cold-season} bias --- but it
inserts a cheaper, better-posed stage ahead of Phase~2.

\textbf{Two objectives, now known to be largely separable} (\S\ref{sub:jjaknob}):
\begin{enumerate}\itemsep1pt
\item \textbf{Surface energy balance $\to0$} to cut spin-up drift. This is an \emph{ocean} surface-flux
problem; the relevant number is $F_\mathrm{sfc}$ over ocean, which has \emph{not} yet been computed --- all
figures quoted so far ($+0.52$ AMIP, $+0.95$ 09C, $+1.77$ 080a) are global. \textbf{Open item.}
\item \textbf{Boreal summer warmth} to restore GDD5 and let trees compete. This is a \emph{land} problem,
caused by an $11$\,W/m$^2$ cloud-driven SW deficit that is absent over ocean (\S\ref{sub:ceres}).
\end{enumerate}

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{l l l l}
\toprule
 & change & where & status / expectation \\
\midrule
\textbf{A} & \texttt{RVRSMIN(3,4)}: $250\to500$ & \texttt{susveg\_mod.F90}:266--267 & \emph{running}; radiatively
neutral; tests partitioning, not the diagnosed cause \\
B & \texttt{RVLAI(3,4)}: $5.0\to4.0$ & \texttt{susveg\_mod.F90}:133--134 & same sign, smaller \\
C & \dead{\texttt{RCLDIFF} $\uparrow$, global} & \dead{\texttt{NAMCLDP}} & \dead{$+$surface SW, but $+$TOA} \\
C$'$ & a \emph{land}-acting cloud lever & convection / BL cloud & the error is land-only, so a global knob
would move the ocean where nothing is wrong \\
D & A\,$+$\,C$'$ & & joint target \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\textbf{Gates:} Siberian JJA bias toward $0$ \emph{and} SW CRE toward CERES; ocean $F_\mathrm{sfc}$ not
degraded; no new error outside the boreal (judge on the global \texttt{part8}/CMPI pattern, \S\ref{sec:cmpi}).
Since the cloud error splits roughly half amount, half opacity (\S\ref{sub:ceres}), a lever acting only on
cloud amount can close at most half of it, and the diagnostic to watch is SW CRE against CERES rather than
temperature alone.

\textbf{Why AMIP is the right testbed.} $2.3$ node-hours per model year against $15.8$ coupled --- a
$\approx7\times$ saving --- and 10\,yr completes in one job. Measured interannual scatter of the boreal JJA
mean is $\sigma=0.25$\,K (Boreal), $0.59$\,K (Siberia), $0.72$\,K (E.\ Siberia), so with 8 usable years the
standard error is $0.09$--$0.26$\,K against biases of $1$--$2$\,K: sampling is not the binding constraint.
\emph{Land spin-up is} --- with \texttt{lresume:\ false} the deep soil (\texttt{stl4}, \texttt{swvl4}) carries
initialisation shock, so discard the first 1--2 years. For paired tuning experiments 5\,yr (1 discarded
$+$ 4 used) suffices, since identical prescribed SST makes most interannual variance cancel in the difference.

\clearpage
\section{Round 10 design: a two-pronged, targeted attack (2026--07--28)}\label{sec:round10}
\emph{Original design as proposed. Operator notes to be appended in \S\ref{sub:r10notes}.}

\textbf{The structural fact the design rests on.} The two largest regional radiation errors have
\emph{opposite} sign at TOA (\S\ref{sub:sobands}, \S\ref{sub:ceres}), so fixing them together is
self-balancing, whereas fixing either alone is not:

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{l l l r}
\toprule
 & error & fixing it & $\Delta$TOA \\
\midrule
\textbf{A1 Southern Ocean} & too \emph{little} cloud ($-6.6$\,pp, SW CRE $+7.8$) & adds cloud, reflects more &
$\mathbf{-0.5}$ to $-0.6$ \\
\textbf{A2 boreal land} & too \emph{much} cloud ($+8.2$\,pp, surface SW $-11$ in JJA) & removes cloud, absorbs more &
$\mathbf{+0.1}$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\subsection{Prong A --- AMIP (atmosphere), A1 before A2}
\textbf{A1 first.} It moves the global target in the right direction ($+0.67\to\approx+0.1$) \emph{and}
attacks the error the campaign has suspected since \S\ref{sec:so}. A2 then costs back only $\approx0.1$ of the
$\approx0.55$ that A1 buys, so the boreal fix becomes affordable --- the ``warm floor'' logic of Part~III,
relocated from the vegetation to the atmosphere.

\textbf{Critical: in AMIP the Southern-Ocean target is \emph{radiation only}, never temperature.}
SST and sea ice are prescribed, so the SO surface temperature cannot respond to a radiation error --- the
excess absorbed shortwave is simply exported through the fixed boundary. \textbf{A1 must therefore be judged
on SW CRE and cloud area against CERES, and it is meaningless to look for an SO temperature response in
AMIP.} This is also why the SO \emph{warm} bias appears only in AWI-CM3/AWI-ESM3: it is a \emph{coupled}
phenomenon, in which the same $+5.8$\,W/m$^2$ of excess absorbed energy has somewhere to go and warms the
ocean. The causal chain is then explicit and closes the compensating-error argument of \S\ref{sec:so}:
\begin{center}\small
AMIP SO cloud deficit ($-6.6$\,pp) $\to$ $+5.8$\,W/m$^2$ excess absorbed $\to$
\emph{(coupled only)} SO warm SST bias $\to$ suppressed by the 06T/06V ocean-mixing levers
\end{center}
i.e.\ Part~I was damping Weddell/Ross convection to compensate an \emph{atmospheric} cloud error. It follows
that the only test of whether A1 fixed the real problem is a \emph{coupled} run --- AMIP can confirm the
radiation is right, but not that the SO warm bias is gone.

\textbf{On levers.} OpenIFS has no land-only cloud knob, and that is informative rather than obstructive: the
land-specificity of A2 ($-16.5$\,W/m$^2$ CRE error over Siberian land against $-2.2$ over ocean at the same
latitude) is itself evidence that the cause is \textbf{land-surface moisture supply}, not the cloud scheme ---
which makes the \texttt{RVRSMIN} sensitivity test (\S\ref{sub:expA}) potentially the actual fix rather than a
proxy for one. A1, being over ocean, is a genuine cloud-scheme / microphysics target.

\textbf{Guardrails on every A-run} --- all four, every time:
\begin{enumerate}\itemsep1pt
\item global net TOA \emph{and} surface flux (state which convention, \S\ref{sub:snowenth});
\item SO 45--65\textdegree S SW CRE and cloud area against CERES --- \emph{radiation only}, since prescribed
SST makes an SO temperature response impossible in AMIP by construction (above);
\item \textbf{the tropics} --- $50\,\%$ of the globe and the largest single contribution ($-1.26$), and the
term the 06O/06T/06V levers degraded (\S\ref{sec:cmpi});
\item CMPI by \emph{pattern}, not the global number.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Prong B --- LPJ-GUESS, and it must go second}
\textbf{Do not tune competition parameters yet.} The tree deficit is largely forcing-driven ($-54\,\%$
Siberian \texttt{TREEFPC} from the forcing swap alone, \S\ref{sub:xfer}), so tuning LPJG while its forcing is
$11$\,W/m$^2$ dim and $2.2$\,K cold would bake the atmospheric error into vegetation parameters. That is the
compensating-error trap, and it is what the 07-series was already doing --- which is the natural reading of
why it achieved nothing (\S\ref{sec:round09}).

\begin{enumerate}\itemsep1pt
\item Complete Prong A.
\item \textbf{Regenerate the LPJG forcing} from the corrected atmosphere. Enabling work required:
\texttt{amip\_pi\_base} uses the default \texttt{file\_def} and emits the evaluation fields but \emph{not} the
daily LPJG forcing set, so a merged \texttt{file\_def} (or a second short run) is needed.
\item \textbf{Re-spin and re-derive the residual.} Whatever tree deficit survives against a corrected forcing
is the real LPJG target. Only then are \texttt{greff\_min} / \texttt{pstemp\_low} legitimate levers.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Gates, replacing the void one.} \dead{FORESTFPC$>0$} is unachievable by construction
(\S\ref{sub:xfer}) and must not be used. Use instead: \texttt{TREEFPC} against the CRUNCEP-arm equilibrium
($0.357$ Siberia, $0.359$ E.\ Siberia); \texttt{AGDD5} recovery toward $776$/$748$; and \texttt{cvh} toward
the MODIS $0.4$--$0.7$ closed-taiga range.

\subsection{Ordering}
\textbf{A1 $\to$ A2 $\to$ regenerate forcing $\to$ re-spin LPJG $\to$ B.} Prong B cannot be evaluated honestly
until Prong A has fixed what feeds it.

\subsection{Operator notes}\label{sub:r10notes}
\emph{(reserved --- to be filled in)}

\section{Round 10 results (2026--07--30): 46-year runs rebuild the ranking}\label{sec:round10res}
All 19 prong-A runs were extended from 6 to 46 years (evaluated 1872--1915, 44\,yr) after a statistical-power
check showed the original 4-year window could not resolve the boreal signal at all.

\subsection{The 4-year window was resolving nothing in the boreal}\label{sub:noisefloor}
A run\,$\times$\,year ANOVA (\texttt{scripts/analysis/noise\_floor.py}) decomposes each per-year value into a
tuning signal, the SST-forced excursion common to every run, and internal noise. At 4 years the 95\,\% detection
threshold on Siberian JJA T2m was $\pm0.89$\,K and \textbf{1 of 18 runs cleared it} --- A1a, the overshoot already
rejected. Siberian JJA surface SW: 1 of 18. Siberian JJA cloud area: \textbf{0 of 18}. The energy target was by
contrast well resolved ($\pm0.23$\,W/m$^2$ on global TOA, 9 of 18 significant), so the campaign's Southern-Ocean
and global conclusions were safe while \emph{every boreal ranking was noise}.

At 44 years the threshold falls to $\pm\mathbf{0.240}$\,K, and the ranking inverts:

\begin{center}\footnotesize
\begin{tabular}{l r r r l}
\toprule
run & 4-yr $\Delta$ & \textbf{44-yr $\Delta$} & $t$ & verdict \\
\midrule
\textbf{B5} \texttt{RCAPDCYCL} $2\to0$   & $+0.001$ & $\mathbf{+0.407}$ & $+3.32$ & \textbf{significant --- best} \\
\textbf{B2} \texttt{RCLDIFF\_CONVI}$=25$ & $+0.290$ & $\mathbf{+0.344}$ & $+2.80$ & \textbf{significant} \\
\textbf{B3} \texttt{RCLDIFF}$=1.5$E-5    & $+0.366$ & $\mathbf{+0.306}$ & $+2.50$ & \textbf{significant} \\
A1a \texttt{ovl}$=0.10$                  & $-1.149$ & $-0.749$ & $-6.11$ & significant, wrong way \\
\textbf{B8} \texttt{RVLAMSK}$=5$         & $\mathbf{+0.502}$ & $\mathbf{-0.038}$ & $-0.31$ & \textbf{noise} \\
AB, B7, C2, E1, A2, A1c, B6              & --- & $\le+0.12$ & --- & noise \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent Two reversals matter. \textbf{B8, promoted to ``best lever of the campaign'' on its $+0.502$\,K, is
noise}; it had already been retracted on 07--29 when the noise floor was first measured, and the 46-year runs
confirm that retraction. \textbf{B5, dismissed as ``inert'' at $+0.001$\,K, is the strongest lever in the
campaign.} The caution recorded at the time --- \emph{unresolved, not proven inert} --- is what kept it in play.

\emph{Method note for future rounds:} B8 was promoted and then built upon twice (ABB8, E1) before its uncertainty
was ever estimated. Three of that round's four runs were spent following a result inside the noise. The noise
floor cost one post-processing script and no node-hours.

\subsection{Period offset, measured rather than estimated}\label{sub:perdirect}
\texttt{amip\_presentday} is the same model and configuration run over 1989--2015, so the epoch offset is
measurable directly instead of chained through observations (\S\ref{sub:perioffset}).

\begin{center}\footnotesize
\begin{tabular}{l r r r r r}
\toprule
arm & Sib JJA T2m & vs ERA5 90--14 & SO SW CRE & vs CERES & net TOA \\
\midrule
control (1872--1915)     & $9.73$  & $\mathbf{-2.45}$ & $-60.29$ & $\mathbf{+7.85}$ & $0.64$ \\
presentday (1990--2014)  & $10.14$ & $\mathbf{-2.04}$ & $-60.36$ & $\mathbf{+7.78}$ & $2.20$ \\
\textbf{measured offset} &         & $\mathbf{+0.42}$ &          & $\mathbf{-0.07}$ & $+1.56$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent Both 07--29 corrections were too large. The boreal offset is $+0.42$\,K, not $+1.12$, so the
period-clean bias is $\mathbf{-2.04}$\,K; the SO SW CRE offset is $-0.07$, not $-1.43$, so that gap is
$\mathbf{+7.78}$ --- essentially the original $+8.08$, and the ``$\approx6$\,W/m$^2$'' claim is withdrawn.

\textbf{The real finding is underneath the error.} The model warms Siberian JJA by only $+0.42$\,K between the
epochs where observations imply $\approx+1.1$\,K, so \textbf{the model under-warms the historical period by
$\approx0.7$\,K} --- a \emph{trend} error distinct from the mean-state bias. Taking observations as correct for
the epoch change puts the 1870s bias at $\approx-1.3$\,K; taking the model's own offset puts it at
$\approx-2.0$\,K. The honest target range is $\mathbf{-1.3}$ \textbf{to} $\mathbf{-2.0}$\,\textbf{K}.

\emph{Caveat:} \texttt{presentday} global net TOA is $+2.20$ against CERES's $+0.97$. Aerosols were verified
correct (MACv2-SP transient, years 1989$\to$2002 logged), so this is a genuine radiative bias rather than a
missing-forcing artefact, consistent with the SO cloud deficit being identical across epochs.

\subsection{The runs were never 1850 GHG --- a dead namelist block}\label{sub:deadnml}
\texttt{NCMIPFIXYR: 1850} never took effect in any \texttt{oifsamip} run. All 24 runscripts hand-wrote a
\texttt{\&NAMECECMIP6} block; OpenIFS 48r1 renamed that namelist to \texttt{\&NAMECECMIP}
(\texttt{ece\_cmip.F90}:68 \texttt{POSNAM}s \texttt{'NAMECECMIP'} only, and \texttt{NAMECECMIP6}/\texttt{SSPNAME}
appear \emph{nowhere} in the source). The legacy block survives in esm-tools' \texttt{fort.4} template, so
\texttt{f90nml} patched it without complaint and the generated namelist looked correct --- while the model read
only \texttt{NAMECECMIP} and left \texttt{NCMIPFIXYR} at its default $-1$. Since
\texttt{LFIXYEAR = NCMIPFIXYR > 0}, forcing was \textbf{transient}. From the model's own output:

\begin{center}\footnotesize\begin{verbatim}
UPDRGAS: greenhouse gas concentrations for YEAR/MONTH  1870/01
CO2 = 286.94 -> 287.12 -> 287.31 -> 287.49 ...   (rising through the run)
SP_SETUP: IYR = 1870, 1871, 1872, ...            (MACv2-SP aerosols advancing)
\end{verbatim}\end{center}

\noindent So every run is a transient CMIP7 \emph{historical} run at its actual calendar year, Krakatoa (1883:
$-0.98$\,K in Siberian JJA) included, not a fixed-1850 PI run.

\textbf{The tuning results are unaffected}: all runs share that identical forcing trajectory, the perturbations
live in \texttt{NAMCLDP}/\texttt{NAMVDF}/source which \emph{are} read, and the ANOVA absorbs the shared trend and
the volcanoes in $g[\mathrm{year}]$. \textbf{The absolute targets are affected}, since they are all read off the
control: CO$_2$ drifts $287\to301$\,ppm ($\approx+0.15$\,W/m$^2$ mid-run vs 1850, partly offset by aerosols) and
the volcanoes depress the 44-yr mean by $\approx0.05$\,K. Small --- but the global TOA target is only
$+0.53$\,W/m$^2$, so $\approx0.1$\,W/m$^2$ of spurious forcing is not negligible \emph{there}.
\texttt{amip\_presentday} is unaffected: \texttt{historical} is \emph{correct} for 1990--2014.

The fix is the setup's own switch, not a hand-patch: \texttt{oifs.scenario: "piControl"} routes through
\texttt{configs/components/oifs/oifs48.cmip.yaml}, which writes \texttt{NCMIPFIXYR} into the live block and sets
the CMIP7 stratospheric-aerosol switches in \texttt{NAERAD}. The dead block has been removed from all 24
runscripts and \texttt{amip\_picontrol} is running as a true PI reference; \texttt{amip\_pi\_base} is
deliberately untouched because all 19 lever deltas reference it. \textbf{The CMIP7 production spin-up is not
affected} --- its \texttt{NCMIPFIXYR}$=1850$ is in the live block and CO$_2$ is pinned at $284.30$\,ppm at
calendar year 2018. \emph{Residual code gap:} nothing pins MACv2-SP tropospheric aerosols
(\texttt{updtim.F90}:812--818 takes the calendar year unconditionally).

\subsection{Can the levers be combined? Not as they stand}\label{sub:combine}
Targets from the 44-year control, and the significant per-lever responses:

\begin{center}\footnotesize
\begin{tabular}{l r r r r}
\toprule
 & SO SW CRE & Sib JJA T2m & global TOA & tropics TOA \\
\midrule
\textbf{required} $\Delta$ & $\mathbf{-7.85}$ & $\mathbf{+1.3}$ to $\mathbf{+2.0}$ & $\mathbf{-0.64}$ & $\mathbf{+2.50}$ \\
\midrule
A1a \texttt{ovl}$=0.10$ & $\mathbf{-6.46}$ & $-0.749$ & $-1.90$ & $-1.07$ \\
A1b \texttt{ovl}$=0.35$ & $-2.17$ & $-0.135$ & $-0.58$ & $-0.29$ \\
B5 & $-0.23$ & $\mathbf{+0.407}$ & $-1.00$ & $\mathbf{-1.94}$ \\
B2 & $+0.11$ & $+0.344$ & $+0.39$ & $+0.65$ \\
B3 & $+0.67$ & $+0.306$ & $+1.34$ & $\mathbf{+2.02}$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent Note the tropics are $2.5$\,W/m$^2$ \emph{too low}, not too high, so B5's $-1.94$ is a real cost and
only B3 helps there. Added linearly:

\begin{center}\footnotesize
\begin{tabular}{l r r r r}
\toprule
stack & SO & Sib & global TOA & tropics \\
\midrule
A1b$+$B5           & $-2.40$ & $+0.27$ & $-0.94$ \ding{55} & $40.4$ \ding{55} \\
A1b$+$B2$+$B5      & $-2.30$ & $+0.62^{*}$ & $-0.55$ \ding{55} & $41.0$ \ding{55} \\
A1b$+$B2$+$B3$+$B5 & $-1.63$ \ding{55} & $+0.92^{*}$ & $+0.79$ \ding{55} & $43.0$ \\
A1a$+$B2$+$B3$+$B5 & $\mathbf{-5.91}$ & $+0.31$ \ding{55} & $-0.53$ & $42.3$ \ding{55} \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent$^{*}$optimistic: AB measured $-0.053$\,K where additivity predicted $+0.21$, so A1b \emph{cancels}
B2's boreal gain.

\textbf{Two structural walls, not bad luck.}
\begin{itemize}\itemsep1pt
\item \textbf{The Southern Ocean is reachable only through A1-strength mixed-phase overlap, which cools Siberia.}
That is the same process on both ends: more supercooled liquid brightens SO cloud
\emph{and} boreal mid-level cloud. A1a buys $82\,\%$ of the SO target and spends $-0.75$\,K of Siberia doing it.
\item \textbf{Siberia's levers are too small and do not add.} The three significant ones sum to $+1.06$\,K
against a $+1.3$--$2.0$\,K need, before the non-additivity that AB demonstrated.
\end{itemize}

\noindent \textbf{The LPJ-GUESS fallback is closed}: \S\ref{sub:radvstemp} showed the forest is $75$--$85\,\%$
temperature-driven, so prong~B cannot absorb a boreal residual the atmosphere does not deliver. The boreal
problem must be solved in OpenIFS.

\section{Round 11 design (2026--07--30): attack the mechanisms, not the parameters}\label{sec:round11}
Round 10 closed on two structural walls (\S\ref{sub:combine}). Round 11 therefore targets the underlying
mechanisms. Two proposals were written and then \textbf{withdrawn for being tuning-to-fit}, and recording why is
part of the result:

\begin{itemize}\itemsep2pt
\item \dead{Land/sea split on \texttt{RCL\_OVERLAPLIQICE}.} \textbf{Anti-physical.} That parameter is the
sub-grid \emph{overlap fraction of supercooled liquid with ice} (range $[0,1]$, default $0.65$, EC-Earth4 uses
$0.1$), entering $\mathtt{ZDEPOS}=\max(\mathtt{ZOVERLAP\_LIQICE}\cdot\mathtt{ZA}\cdot(\mathtt{ZINEW}-\mathtt{ZICE0}),0)$.
The code's own comment states the intended selectivity --- \emph{``Reduce in shallow convection because assume
SLW in active updraught is less overlapped with ice in less active part''} --- i.e.\ convective segregation
\emph{lowers} overlap. But ocean mixed-phase cloud is predominantly stratiform (well mixed, so \emph{high}
overlap) and land cloud more convective (segregated, \emph{low} overlap), the \textbf{opposite} of what the
tuning wants.
\item \dead{\texttt{RCAPDCYCL} mode 3 = disable the CAPE diurnal-cycle correction over land.}
\textbf{A regression.} That correction is Bechtold et al.\ (2014), which exists to fix the documented error of
land convection triggering too early in the day.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{D1 --- \texttt{RCAPDCYCL} mode 4: reformulate the land closure}\label{sub:d1}
\textbf{B5 was misdiagnosed.} \texttt{RCAPDCYCL} is a \emph{mode selector}, not a magnitude
(\texttt{cumastrn.F90}:773,777): $1.0$ = land only, scaled on \texttt{ZKHVFL} (surface kinematic virtual heat
flux, \texttt{:464}); $2.0$ = everywhere, land via $\mathtt{ZCAPPBL}\cdot\mathtt{ZTAU}/\mathtt{ZTAURES}$ and
ocean via a wind-based \texttt{ZTAUPBL}; $0.0$ = off entirely. So B5's $2.0\to0.0$ \textbf{deleted the
correction globally}: its $+0.407$\,K boreal gain came from the land branch, its $-1.94$\,W/m$^2$ tropical cost
from losing the ocean branch. The two are separable.

The physical argument is in the scaling. Mode 1 is proportional to the \emph{actual surface buoyancy flux} and
vanishes where that forcing is weak; mode 2's land branch uses boundary-layer CAPE instead. Boreal summer has
weak insolation, a shallow boundary layer and small surface fluxes, so the buoyancy-flux form self-limits while
the BL-CAPE form need not --- a real reason to suspect \textbf{mode 2's land branch is mis-scaled for
weak-forcing high-latitude convection}, and it predicts the observed sign. \textbf{Mode 4} (added this build)
is mode-1 land physics with mode-2's ocean branch verbatim; both are existing code. \emph{Prediction:} recovers
part of $+0.407$\,K with little of the tropical cost. If the boreal gain vanishes too, the effect was never
about the land closure formulation and the B5 result needs re-reading.

\subsection{D2 --- ice nuclei, not overlap}\label{sub:d2}
The Southern-Ocean supercooled-liquid deficit is an \textbf{ice-nucleation} problem, and the scheme has no
aerosol dependence whatsoever:
\[
\mathtt{ZICENUCLEI} = 1000\cdot\exp(12.96\cdot\mathtt{ZSUPERSATICE}-0.639)
\qquad(\texttt{cloudsc.F90}:2516,\ :2618)
\]
That is \textbf{Meyers et al.\ (1992)} --- a function of ice supersaturation \emph{only}, documented to
overestimate INP in clean environments, which is precisely the remote Southern Ocean; DeMott et al.\
(2010/2015) make INP a function of aerosol number instead. Reducing INP over ocean therefore has a real
mechanism (INP sources are continental: mineral dust, biological) with the correct sign in both regions at
once, and strong leverage since $\mathtt{ZCVDS}\propto\mathtt{ZICENUCLEI}^{0.666}$ --- a factor $0.1$ cuts
deposition to $\approx0.22$.

\textbf{Three better routes are closed.} (i) \texttt{PNICE} is already a \texttt{cloudsc} argument
(\texttt{:265}) and already used for autoconversion (\texttt{:2855}), but it is filled only when
$\mathtt{NAERCLD}>0$ (\texttt{callpar.F90}:1155, else zeroed at \texttt{:1166}) and \texttt{NAERCLD} defaults
to $0$; that configuration is several times slower and \textbf{would not finish CMIP7 in time}.
(ii) \texttt{onlinedust\_v4\_rmp.nc} is staged and \emph{opened} unconditionally, but its arrays
(\texttt{POTSRC}, \texttt{SOILTYPE}, \texttt{CULT}) are allocated only by \texttt{TM5M7\_SRC\_DUST\_INIT} via
\texttt{tm5m7\_init.F90} --- \textbf{only under m7} --- so its contents are never populated here
(\texttt{suecrad}'s \texttt{YDUSTCLIM} is a different object, dust \emph{optical properties} for the radiation
climatology). (iii) MACv2-SP plumes are anthropogenic sulphate/BC/OC: poor INP, sulphate coating
\emph{deactivates} mineral dust INP, and in a piControl anthropogenic aerosol is near zero, giving no
land/ocean contrast.

\subsubsection{Two physical caveats that shape the test}\label{sub:d2caveats}
\textbf{The tropics will pay, and ``ocean'' is the wrong discriminator.} The scaling acts only inside the
mixed-phase window ($\mathtt{RTHOMO}\le T<\mathtt{RTT}-5$, i.e.\ $235.15$--$268.15$\,K, with liquid present),
which in the tropics sits at $\approx500$--$300$\,hPa and \emph{is} populated --- deep convective updraughts
carry supercooled liquid well above the freezing level, and anvil detrainment feeds it. Reducing INP there
retains more supercooled liquid, brightening tropical mid-level cloud and pushing tropical TOA \emph{further
below} CERES. Expect a cost of order A1a's $-1.07$\,W/m$^2$ or worse. More fundamentally, tropical and
subtropical oceans are \emph{not} dust-remote (Saharan dust over the tropical Atlantic, Asian dust over the
North Pacific); the Southern Ocean is uniquely far from continental sources. The property that distinguishes it
is \textbf{remoteness}, which a local land-fraction mask cannot encode.

\textbf{Sea salt sets an INP floor.} Sea salt itself is a poor INP (highly soluble, inefficient in the immersion
mode at these temperatures), but sea spray carries \textbf{marine biogenic organics} --- sea-surface microlayer
material, phytoplankton exudates --- which are a modest INP source (Wilson et al.\ 2015; DeMott et al.\ 2016).
So the ocean INP factor \textbf{must not approach zero}: $\approx0.1$--$0.3$ is defensible, not $0.01$. Sea
salt's larger role is as CCN, which is moot here since \texttt{PCCN} is zeroed with $\mathtt{NAERCLD}=0$ and
droplet number is the constant \texttt{RCL\_KK\_CLOUD\_NUM\_SEA}. A tempering caveat: marine biogenic INP peaks
with the austral-summer bloom (DJF), exactly when SO insolation and our SW bias peak, so the SO is
\emph{least} INP-starved in the season we care most about.

\subsection{The three runs}\label{sub:round11runs}
All on the same forcing as the 19 round-10 levers (\texttt{oifs.scenario} defaults to \texttt{historical}), so
deltas are directly comparable to B5's; 46\,yr, evaluated 1872--1915.

\begin{center}\footnotesize
\begin{tabular}{l l l}
\toprule
run & change & purpose \\
\midrule
\textbf{D1} & \texttt{RCAPDCYCL}$=4$ (source + namelist) & separate B5's boreal gain from its tropical cost \\
\textbf{D2a} & \texttt{ZICENUCLEI} $\times f(\mathtt{PLSM})$, no remoteness & does the INP route have leverage at all? \\
\textbf{D2b} & tile-based two-term INP $+$ remoteness gate & the physically honest version \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent D2a is run \emph{knowing} it should damage the tropics: the question it answers is whether INP has
Southern-Ocean leverage, which decides whether D2b is worth building. D2b computes the INP field in
\texttt{callpar} from \texttt{SURFL\%ZFRTI} (tile fractions, already used at \texttt{callpar.F90}:2006) and
\texttt{PAUX\%PGEMU} (sine of latitude, available at \texttt{:1801}), passing one new array to \texttt{cloudsc}:
\[
\mathrm{INP}\ \propto\ a\cdot[\text{bare soil (tile 8)}\times\text{wind}]\ +\ b\cdot[\text{low veg (4)}+\text{high veg (6)}]
\]
Tile convention from \texttt{srfi\_mod.F90}:52. The two terms matter: a \emph{dust-only} proxy would give the
\textbf{wrong sign} over boreal forest, which is a weak dust source --- lowering its INP would brighten boreal
cloud and cool it further. Vegetated land is instead a strong \textbf{biological} INP source in summer (pollen,
bacteria, fungal spores, litter), peaking in exactly our season, while snow tiles (5/7) collapse that term in
winter and leave the cold-season bias untouched.

All three are source edits on the single \texttt{oifsamip-cy48} tree and therefore \textbf{serialise}: edit,
\texttt{esm\_master recomp-oifsamip-cy48}, verify the binary md5 changed, submit, wait for staging, repeat.

\section{Round 11 results: D2b adopted for the Southern Ocean, D1 falsified}\label{sec:round11res}
\begin{center}\footnotesize
\begin{tabular}{l r r r r r}
\toprule
 & SO SW CRE & SO SW RMSE & Sib JJA T2m & global TOA & tropics \\
\midrule
control                    & $-60.29$ & $6.88$ & $9.73$ & $0.64$ & $42.61$ \\
piCTRL 1850                & $-60.65$ & $6.83$ & $9.74$ & $\mathbf{0.79}$ & $42.69$ \\
D1 \texttt{RCAPDCYCL}$=4$  & $-60.38$ & $6.51$ & $\mathbf{9.56}$ & $0.61$ & $42.53$ \\
D2a INP, no gate           & $\mathbf{-65.13}$ & $5.07$ & $9.56$ & $-0.28$ & $42.23$ \\
\textbf{D2b INP + p700}    & $-62.93$ & $\mathbf{4.96}$ & $9.51$ & $0.36$ & $\mathbf{42.70}$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent\textbf{D2b is adopted as the Southern-Ocean lever.} It gives the best SO surface-SW
RMSE of any run ($6.88\to4.96$, $-28\,\%$) at \emph{zero} tropical cost ($+0.09$), beating both
A1b ($5.54$) and the ungated D2a ($5.07$) --- the latter despite a larger mean-CRE change,
because D2b improves the spatial \emph{pattern}, which is what the priority metric exists to
measure. The ice-nucleation hypothesis (\S\ref{sub:d2}) is therefore supported, and the
pressure gate did what it was designed to do: D2a's tropical cost ($-0.38$) vanishes ($+0.09$)
while $55\,\%$ of the SO gain is retained.

\textbf{D1's prediction is excluded, but as a NULL, not a sign reversal.} It was predicted to recover part of B5's $+0.407$\,K by
switching the land CAPE closure to mode-1's buoyancy-flux scaling; it delivered
$\mathbf{-0.170}$\,K --- which does \emph{not} clear the $\pm0.242$\,K detection floor
(\S\ref{sub:noisefloor}), so the predicted recovery is excluded but the sign is unresolved.
Quoting it as ``the wrong sign'' was the very error \S\ref{sub:noisefloor} exists to prevent. So B5's boreal gain requires \emph{removing} the land
correction, not rescaling it --- the effect is not about which quantity the closure scales on.
Stating the prediction in advance is what made the refutation unambiguous.

\textbf{\texttt{amip\_picontrol} settles two things.} The energy target is $+0.79$\,W/m$^2$,
not $+0.64$ --- slightly \emph{larger}, so A1b does not overshoot. And Siberian JJA T2m differs
by only $+0.008$\,K, so the dead-namelist forcing bug (\S\ref{sub:deadnml}) never affected the
boreal target and every round-10 boreal conclusion stands.

\section{Round 12: boreal surface exchange, and the best lever of the campaign}\label{sec:round12}
Round 11 left the boreal unsolved. Round 12 changed target from convection and cloud to
\textbf{boreal surface exchange}, anchored on one observation: the measured Siberian JJA Bowen
ratio is $\mathbf{0.43}$ against $\approx0.5$--$1.5$ observed for boreal conifer, so the model
puts too much surface energy into latent heat. Four levers, each raising Bowen by an
independent route, all indexed on vegetation types 3/4 so that types 5/6/18 --- and hence the
tropics --- are untouched \emph{by construction}. That is exactly the property B5 lacks.

\begin{center}\footnotesize
\begin{tabular}{l l r r l}
\toprule
run & change & $\Delta$T2m & $t$ & verdict \\
\midrule
\textbf{F4} & \texttt{RVRSMIN(3,4)} $250\to1000$ & $\mathbf{+0.749}$ & $+6.06$ & \textbf{significant, best ever} \\
\textbf{F5} & all four combined                  & $\mathbf{+0.746}$ & $+6.03$ & significant, $\equiv$ F4 \\
B5          & (previous best)                    & $+0.407$ & $+3.29$ & significant \\
F2          & \texttt{RVLAI(3,4)} $5\to3$        & $+0.321$ & $+2.59$ & significant \\
F1          & \texttt{RVZ0H(3,4)}$=$\texttt{z0m}$/10$ & $+0.185$ & $+1.49$ & marginal \\
F3          & \texttt{RVCOV(3,4)} $0.9\to0.7$    & $\approx0$ & --- & noise \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent\textbf{F4 costs the tropics nothing} ($42.68$ vs control $42.61$) where B5 wrecked
them ($40.67$), and it gives the best global T2m RMSE of any run ($1.54$). Bowen moves
$0.44\to0.60$, into the observed range \emph{without} overshooting --- the risk named in
advance did not materialise.

\textbf{F5 equals F4 to $0.003$\,K.} Against a naive sum of $\approx1.25$\,K, adding F1, F2 and
F3 on top of F4 contributes nothing. The advance prediction --- $\text{F5}\ll\sum$, closer to
the largest single lever --- held, and more strongly than expected: near-total saturation of a
single shared latent-heat pathway with a floor at zero. \emph{Use F4 alone.}

\textbf{A prediction that failed.} F1 was called strongest a priori because $kB^{-1}=0$ is the
largest departure from observation (every closed-forest type sets $z_{0h}=z_{0m}$, i.e.\ $kB^{-1}=0$, where observations give $kB^{-1}\approx2$). It is marginal and barely moved Bowen
($0.44\to0.43$). ``Largest departure from observation'' is not a reliable guide to leverage.

\textbf{B5 carries a hidden $-0.72$\,K DJF cooling} (F4: $+0.06$), invisible for the whole
campaign because only JJA was ever evaluated. Given that the coupled model's original
complaint is a \emph{cold-season} bias, that is disqualifying for B5, and DJF now belongs in
the standard guardrails.

\subsection{Is F4 fixing a cause or a symptom?}\label{sub:f4cause}
Two tests, both partly exonerating. \emph{Seasonal selectivity:} DJF $+0.061$, MAM $+0.047$,
JJA $\mathbf{+0.749}$, SON $+0.110$ --- exactly the signature a transpiration mechanism must
have, since stomata are shut and the canopy snow-covered in winter. \emph{Vertical targeting:}
F4 warms $1000/925/850$\,hPa by $+0.83/+0.84/+0.83$, closing $56$--$83\,\%$ of the
\emph{Siberia-specific} layer bias (\S\ref{sub:vprof}), and only $+0.53$ aloft.

What remains true is that \texttt{RVRSMIN} $250\to1000$ is an unanchored $4\times$ excursion
from what ECMWF ships, and that the vertical profiles show it removes moisture that is already
deficient. \textbf{G1 $=$ F4 $+$ D2b} tested this: the first pairing whose components are
disjoint in both process and geography, hence expected not to conflict --- but AB and ABB8 both
got superposition wrong in sign, so it was measured, not assumed. It superposes
(\S\ref{sub:g1}).

\subsection{G1 $=$ F4 $+$ D2b: the best configuration of the campaign}\label{sub:g1}
G1 is the first combination to improve \emph{both} targets at once, and the first whose
superposition is additive rather than wrong in sign.

\begin{center}\footnotesize
\begin{tabular}{l r r r r}
\toprule
metric & control & G1 & $\Delta$ & target \\
\midrule
\textbf{SO SW RMSE} (priority) & $6.877$ & $\mathbf{4.809}$ & $-2.067$ & --- \\
SO TOA SW CRE [W\,m$^{-2}$] & $-60.29$ & $-63.13$ & $-2.84$ & $-68.14$ \\
SO cloud area [\%] & $83.07$ & $83.59$ & $+0.52$ & $89.72$ \\
\textbf{Siberia JJA T2m} [$^\circ$C] & $9.73$ & $\mathbf{10.25}$ & $\mathbf{+0.521}$ ($t{=}4.22$) & $\approx 12.2$ \\
Siberia sfc net SW [W\,m$^{-2}$] & $153.78$ & $159.31$ & $+5.54$ & $166.26$ \\
global net TOA [W\,m$^{-2}$] & $+0.64$ & $+0.45$ & $-0.195$ & $\sim 0$ \\
tropics net TOA [W\,m$^{-2}$] & $42.61$ & $42.71$ & $+0.096$ & $45.11$ \\
global T2m RMSE [K] & $1.579$ & $1.553$ & $-0.026$ & --- \\
Nordic Seas SW RMSE & $9.058$ & $9.358$ & $\mathbf{+0.300}$ & --- \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent\textbf{Superposition, measured:} Siberia JJA T2m F4 $+0.749$ $+$ D2b $-0.222$
$=+0.527$ predicted vs $\mathbf{+0.521}$ actual; SO SW RMSE $-2.131$ vs $-2.067$; SO CRE
$-2.719$ vs $-2.840$; global net TOA $-0.188$ vs $-0.195$. Additive to within noise on every
metric. The plausible reason it holds here and failed for AB/ABB8: F4 acts on a land surface
flux in boreal summer, D2b on ice nucleation over ocean below 700\,hPa, whereas AB and ABB8
combined levers modifying the \emph{same} cloud scheme in the \emph{same} regime.

\noindent\textbf{The one cost} is the Nordic Seas, $+0.300$ SW RMSE against a $\pm0.052$
threshold, and worse than additive ($+0.184$ expected). No other lever in the campaign has
ever moved Nordic RMSE; this one moves it the wrong way. Small box, not the deep-water
priority, but it must not be allowed to grow.

G1 closes $26\,\%$ of the boreal bias and $36\,\%$ of the SO CRE gap. The SO
\emph{cloud-area} deficit of ${\sim}6$\,pp remains untouched by every lever tried.

\subsection{Seasonal audit: the noise floor is not the same in every season}\label{sub:seasonal}
The campaign evaluated JJA only for eleven rounds, while the coupled model's original
complaint is a \emph{cold-season} bias. \texttt{scripts/analysis/seasonal\_by\_run.py} applies
the run$\times$year ANOVA of \S\ref{sub:noisefloor} separately to each season. The detection
threshold varies by a factor of $2.4$ across the year:

\begin{center}\footnotesize
\begin{tabular}{l r r r}
\toprule
season & control & sd$(\epsilon)$ & 95\,\% threshold \\
\midrule
DJF & $-29.35\,^\circ$C & $1.407$\,K & $\pm 0.588$\,K \\
MAM & $-9.28\,^\circ$C & $0.923$\,K & $\pm 0.386$\,K \\
JJA & $+9.73\,^\circ$C & $0.580$\,K & $\pm 0.242$\,K \\
SON & $-10.41\,^\circ$C & $1.031$\,K & $\pm 0.431$\,K \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent Applying the JJA threshold to a winter delta overstates significance by $2.4\times$,
and a first pass through these results did exactly that, briefly recording D2b ($-0.455$) and
B3 ($-0.486$) as winter-damaged. Both are \emph{within} the DJF noise floor. Corrected:

\begin{itemize}\itemsep2pt
\item \textbf{B5 \texttt{capdcycl0} is the only lever with genuine winter damage}
  (DJF $-0.720$, clears $\pm0.588$) and the only one that significantly warms JJA while
  significantly cooling DJF. Its rejection stands, now properly thresholded.
\item \textbf{D2b's real seasonal cost is spring, not winter}: MAM $-0.467$ clears
  $\pm0.386$; its DJF $-0.455$ does not clear $\pm0.588$.
\item \textbf{F4 is seasonally clean} --- DJF $+0.061$, MAM $+0.047$, SON $+0.110$, all within
  noise, the entire signal in JJA. A further argument for F4 over the cloud levers.
\item \textbf{G1 is seasonally clean}: DJF $+0.070$, MAM $-0.296$, SON $+0.280$, none
  significant; D2b's MAM cooling is diluted below threshold.
\item A1a warms DJF/SON and cools MAM/JJA by large amounts --- a global cloud change, not a
  boreal lever. B6, B7 and F3 cool MAM significantly; F5 inherits F3's MAM $-0.507$.
\end{itemize}

\section{Vertical structure vs ERA5: where the biases live}\label{sec:vprof}
\subsection{The Siberia-specific cold bias is confined below 850\,hPa}\label{sub:vprof}
Model minus ERA5 on the model's 19 pressure levels, \texttt{amip\_presentday} vs ERA5
1990--2014 so the comparison is period-clean. ERA5 comes from the DKRZ pool; all 19 model
levels exist exactly in ERA5's 37, so no interpolation is needed.

\begin{center}\footnotesize
\begin{tabular}{r r r r}
\toprule
hPa & Siberia & Global & \textbf{Siberia-specific} \\
\midrule
1000 & $-1.90$ & $-0.73$ & $\mathbf{-1.17}$ \\
 925 & $-2.17$ & $-0.65$ & $\mathbf{-1.52}$ \\
 850 & $-1.99$ & $-0.99$ & $\mathbf{-1.00}$ \\
 700 & $-1.22$ & $-1.15$ & $-0.07$ \\
 500 & $-1.67$ & $-1.49$ & $-0.18$ \\
 300 & $-2.32$ & $-2.22$ & $-0.10$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent This separates two problems that had been conflated. Above 700\,hPa Siberia merely
shares a \textbf{global tropospheric cold bias of $0.7$--$2.9$\,K} that no boreal lever will
touch; the boreal-specific excess lives entirely in the bottom three levels, which is the
signature a surface-exchange problem should have and which validates the F-series framing.

\textbf{The excess boreal cloud is thermally driven, not moisture-driven.} Siberian RH is
$+5.6$ to $+8.2\,\%$ too high while $q$ is \emph{too low} ($-0.2$ to $-0.3$\,g/kg): a 2\,K cold
bias cuts $q_{sat}$ by $\approx14\,\%$ while $q$ falls only $\approx6\,\%$. So a large
F-response must not be read as confirming the cause.

\textbf{Independent support for D2:} the Southern Ocean has RH $+5.8$ to $+6.6\,\%$ too high
through the mixed-phase layer while reflecting \emph{too little} SW --- humidity present,
liquid absent, exactly the supercooled-liquid deficit the INP hypothesis predicts.

\emph{Broken diagnostic, recorded:} the model's pressure-level relative humidity \texttt{r} is
\textbf{identically zero in every run checked} ($0$ nonzero of $17\,510\,400$ values) while
\texttt{q} is fine. The XIOS \texttt{r} output yields silent garbage and must not be used; RH
above is recomputed from \texttt{t} and \texttt{q}.

\subsection{Extending the column downward: the soil is the least-biased part}\label{sub:soilcol}
\S\ref{sub:vprof} stops at 1000\,hPa. Continuing into the skin and the four soil layers
(\texttt{scripts/analysis/vertical\_bias\_column.py}), JJA, model \texttt{amip\_presentday}
minus ERA5. Low-lying land ($<500$\,m) is shown alongside because the all-land figure is
inflated by orographic mismatch (\S\ref{sub:tilebias}):

\begin{center}\footnotesize
\begin{tabular}{l r r r}
\toprule
JJA & global land & land $<500$\,m & Siberian box \\
\midrule
700\,hPa & $-0.83$ & $-0.89$ & $-1.22$ \\
850\,hPa & $-0.79$ & $-0.92$ & $-1.99$ \\
925\,hPa & $-0.82$ & $-1.02$ & $-2.17$ \\
\textbf{2\,m} & $-1.34$ & $\mathbf{-1.00}$ & $-2.04$ \\
skin & $-0.83$ & $\mathbf{-0.53}$ & $-1.66$ \\
soil L1 (7\,cm) & $-0.40$ & $\mathbf{-0.36}$ & $-1.73$ \\
soil L2 (28\,cm) & $-0.42$ & $-0.38$ & $-1.90$ \\
soil L3 (1\,m) & $-0.78$ & $-0.72$ & $-2.14$ \\
soil L4 (2.9\,m) & $-1.09$ & $-0.89$ & $-1.02$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent Over ordinary low-lying land the \textbf{soil and skin are the least-biased parts of
the entire column}, and 2\,m sits about $0.5$\,K \emph{below} the skin under it. The ground is
not what holds the near-surface cold; if anything it is doing better than the air above it.
Siberia is the exception that \S\ref{sub:vprof} already identified --- there the soil is cold
too ($-1.7$ to $-2.1$\,K), which is the surface-confined excess the F-series was built to
attack.

\emph{Caveats.} The 1000\,hPa row is unreliable over land, where much of that level is below
ground and both datasets extrapolate; 925/850 are the lowest honest levels. ERA5's soil and
skin are HTESSEL output, so those rows are model-versus-sibling rather than observation --- the
2\,m and pressure-level rows are the observation-constrained ones.

\subsection{The near-surface cold is not boreal: every surface type is cold}\label{sub:tilebias}
The campaign has only ever scored one box, so it had never checked whether its levers address a
local fault or a global one. Resolving the 2\,m bias by dominant surface type
(\texttt{scripts/analysis/bias\_by\_tile.py}) gives an unambiguous answer:
\textbf{all 20 surface types are cold, in every season. Not one is warm.}

\begin{center}\footnotesize
\begin{tabular}{l r r r r r}
\toprule
dominant type & area & DJF & MAM & JJA & SON \\
\midrule
bare soil & $25.3\,\%$ & $-1.86$ & $-2.12$ & $-2.16$ & $-2.03$ \\
Evgr Broadleaf (tropical) & $8.7\,\%$ & $-1.78$ & $-1.79$ & $-1.46$ & $-1.42$ \\
Tundra & $5.2\,\%$ & $-2.51$ & $-0.18$ & $-1.43$ & $-1.84$ \\
Evgr Needleleaf & $4.7\,\%$ & $-2.00$ & $-1.84$ & $-1.16$ & $-1.05$ \\
Dec Needleleaf & $3.0\,\%$ & $-1.22$ & $-1.55$ & $-1.87$ & $-1.69$ \\
\midrule
\textbf{ALL LAND} & $100\,\%$ & $\mathbf{-1.73}$ & $\mathbf{-1.60}$ & $\mathbf{-1.35}$ & $\mathbf{-1.39}$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent \textbf{Two controls bound how much of this is real.} The land figure is
\emph{elevation-dependent} --- $-0.70$\,K below 200\,m rising to $-2.43$\,K above 2000\,m, the
expected signature of the model's smoothed orography against ERA5's 25\,km --- so the all-land
number overstates it and the low-lying value is the honest one. And over \textbf{prescribed-SST
ocean} 2\,m is $-0.72$\,K: about $0.7$\,K of near-surface cold exists where the land surface is
absent entirely.

\textbf{Consequence for the campaign.} Siberia's JJA $-2.58$\,K decomposes roughly as
${\sim}0.7$\,K universal, ${\sim}0.3$\,K orographic, and $1.3$--$1.6$\,K boreal-specific. G4 has
taken ${\sim}0.95$\,K of that boreal budget --- which is a far better explanation of the
F-series saturation (F5 $=$ F4 to within noise, \S\ref{sec:round12}) than any parameter limit.
\emph{The boreal budget is nearly spent, and the remainder is not reachable by a
boreal-indexed lever.}

\textbf{One benign consequence.} Because every type is cold, a lever indexed by vegetation type
does not damage the regions it leaks into: the non-boreal areas of types 4 and 9 are cold too
(JJA $-0.68$ and $-1.06$). That is what licenses the round-17 restriction to those two types
(\S\ref{sec:lamsk}).

\section{Surface albedo: half the Siberian SW deficit is not cloud}\label{sec:albedo}
Every boreal lever so far has attacked cloud. Decomposed against CERES on the identical box
and mask, the $12.5$\,W/m$^2$ Siberian JJA surface-SW deficit is only half cloud:
$\mathbf{7.0}$\,W/m$^2$ from too little SW reaching the surface, and
$\mathbf{5.5}$\,W/m$^2$ from \textbf{surface albedo too high} --- $0.1753$ model versus
$0.1461$ CERES, entirely independent of cloud. F4 does not touch it ($0.1749$).

\subsection{Not a global albedo error, and not the forest}\label{sub:albreg}
Temperate and tropical land is essentially perfect (Europe, Amazon, Great Plains, steppe,
Australia all within $\pm0.004$), so the albedo \emph{scheme} is sound and a global fix would
be wrong. \emph{This held up, with one amendment.} \S\ref{sub:snowtile} later measured land
albedo globally and by type with snow masked per cell per month: every forest type is inside
$+0.006$ and tropical broadleaf is at $-0.0003$, confirming the scheme --- but a residual
$+0.0080$ does exist on crops, semidesert and bare soil, so ``a global fix would be wrong''
is right about the \emph{vegetation table} and too strong about the \emph{soil background}.
The error is concentrated at high latitude: Siberian tundra $+0.094$ ($-16.0$\,W/m$^2$),
Canada boreal $+0.032$, Siberia boreal $+0.029$, Sahara $+0.020$ --- but \textbf{Fennoscandia,
the same needleleaf type, is correct at $+0.002$}. Splitting Siberia by latitude gives $+0.011$
in the forest core and $+0.087$ at 70--75N.

\subsection{How the albedo is built, and two hypotheses that died}\label{sub:albbuild}
